2025-06-15T01:20:28.9007229Z *This section includes details on the pull request with code changes you have already submitted to fix the problem above.*
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9008699Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9008953Z <pr_title>
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9009899Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9011069Z </pr_title>
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9011359Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9011572Z <pr_description>
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9012788Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9013943Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9014066Z ## Problem
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9014746Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9015387Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9015790Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9016220Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9016642Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9017063Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9017421Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9017609Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9018329Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9019213Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9019338Z ## Solution
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9019507Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9019657Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9020413Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9020816Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9021580Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9022850Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9024070Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9024804Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9024977Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9025521Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9025844Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9026020Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9026439Z ```bash
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9027410Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9028547Z ```
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9028693Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9028849Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9029230Z ```bash
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9030420Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9031844Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9032273Z ```
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9032452Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9032725Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9033187Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9033362Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9034769Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9035768Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9035862Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9036139Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9036681Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9037245Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9037779Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9038394Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9038740Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9039162Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9039685Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9039771Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9039883Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9039887Z 
2025-06-15T01:20:28.9039989Z </pr_description>
